Preface 

The present era has created complications in respect to living and death settlements, which require more than strictly legal skill to enable a lawyer to draft documents that will reflect the proper wishes of the maker, produce results equitable to the beneficiaries and at the same time adequately protect society. 

Text books treat exhaustively principles of law from the standpoint of court decisions and statutory enactments, but information for the actual drawing of documents relating to property dispositions effective before, at or after death, in order properly to anticipate changing conditions can be supplied to better advantage through the use of form books that can be kept up to date at considerably less expense, which explains the purpose of this publication. 

It is hoped that busy lawyers will find this hand book, now in its third edition, helpful as a means of suggesting thoughts and phrases to be incorporated in wills and trust documents. These forms have been accumulated and prepared out of necessity by the author in his practice and are drawn from the adjudicated wills of prominent people, from textbooks, form books and other sources believed to be authoritative and founded upon sound legal principles. 

They primarily conform to Ohio Law and practice, yet in a majority of cases are adaptable for use in every state in the Union. In view of what has been stated, lawyers are cautioned to test their validity according to the law of the particular jurisdiction where the document is to become operative. Laymen should be discouraged from using this book except for the development of ideas concerning possible methods of estate distribution. If any plan, form or clause appears suitable, he should counsel with his lawyer as to its legality under the law of his domicile and where his property is situated. No will or trust should be drawn by anyone but an expert. 

Criticism by members of the Bar and the submission of additional forms is invited as it will be of material assistance to the author in the preparation of any subsequent edition.

Suggestions to Lawyers for Drawing Wills and Trusts 2N 1. The testator should submit to his lawyer, full information concerning those to whom he owes natural bounty; and in this connection it is well to obtain the following information: 

Names of wife and children and their ages. 

Physical and mental conditions of members of family. 

Are there any children not of the blood of the testator and have they been legally adopted? 

Facts as to intended beneficiaries, not members of the immediate family. 

Is there a divorced wife or husband living? 

Are there any living children of testator by previous marriage? 

Are any of the children married? With or without issue? 

2. The testator should divulge full information as to all property he possesses, by what title he holds the same, whether it is encumbered by mortgage or otherwise and each piece of property should be accurately described. 

3. The purpose and the manner in which he desires his property to be distributed should be carefully analyzed and the lawyer should point out the probable consequences of any unjust or unequal distribution. 

4. The lawyer should explain to the testator the claims that the law imposes upon him concerning his property dispositions which he has no power to alter by last will and testament and explain in non-technical language any legal requirements necessary for the proper administration of his estate. 

The lawyer should obtain all necessary data to report to his testator the taxes his estate will have to pay under the laws of the federal government and the various states in which he owns property and suggest plans for their elimination or reduction, as the case may be. 

6. The financial needs of the family should be considered during the period of administration. When should income to wife begin? In determining this question, consideration should be given to separate income and property already belonging to wife. 

7. Full information should be obtained concerning the testator's obligations, both direct and contingent and careful consideration should be given to the development of a plan for their payment. 

8. If particular funds are set apart for the payment of debts, it should be clearly stated whether it is the intention of the testator to thereby exonerate the general personalty from being primarily liable to such charge. 

9. If the nature of the present estate of the testator is such as to indicate the possibility of shrinkage in value of assets, consideration should be given to the subject of abatement of legacies; also the possibility of lapsing should be anticipated. 

10. If the testator operates a partnership, or a close corporation in which he holds a majority interest, he must consider what shall be done with the business. If he desires it to be continued, for how long and under what restrictions? Should the executor be empowered to borrow money for the business? The question of providing suitable management should also be considered. 

11. What is to be done with the homestead or other real estate? If there is a mortgage on the devised property should it constitute a charge against the corpus of the estate or be assumed by the devisee? 

If real estate is sold by the testator after the execution of the will and before his death what devise or legacy should be given in lieu thereof? What should be done with lapsed devises? 

12. Consideration should be given to the matter of creating trusts. What property should be put into the trust? Who are to be the beneficiaries? If the wife is a beneficiary, when should she begin to receive income? How frequently should it be paid to her? What encroachment powers should be granted and by whom? What is to be done if she remarries? What disposition should be made of the trust upon her death? If the children of the testator are to be beneficiaries, under what circumstances should they receive principal? Who should receive the income during the minority of the children? How often should it be [5]

paid to them? How long should the trusts run for the benefit of the children? Should it be paid to them in a lump sum or in installments upon attaining various ages? Should there be a spendthrift clause to prevent creditors attacking income or principal settlements? 

13. Instructions necessary for the preparation of a Will should be taken from the testator himself rather than from third persons, particularly where such persons are related or will be named beneficiaries. 

The will should be written on one page of paper or if several sheets are used, they should be attached so. as to constitute one entirety. 

14. If any interlineations are made, notations should be made in writing opposite same to the effect that they were made before the will was signed by the testator. 

15. In drafting the will he should be careful to see that all words used in the document are understood by the testator and that they are such that the law will properly construe to carry into effect the testator's real intentions. 

16. In the execution of the will, the lawyer should see that the testator signs his name at the end; that the necessary witnesses attest the signature and that the witnesses either see the testator sign his name or hear him acknowledge that the signature is his own and he must further see that the testator informs the witnesses that the paper which they are attesting is his Last Will and Testament. 

17. He should ascertain from the witnesses whether they regard the testator to be in his right mind and that he understands the document to be his Last Will and Testament, as well as the fact that the will is made without restraint. 

18. POWERS OF EXECUTOR. To appoint a person or corporation executor of an estate without defining his responsibilities and powers is like asking someone to row a boat without oars. If worthy of appointment, the executor should be empowered to compromise claims, discharge debts, and sell real and personal property at public or private sale, without order of court. If the estate is encumbered, power should be given to the executor to renew notes, extend indebtedness and borrow money. If the testator is interested in a partnership or close corporation, express powers should be made in the Will for its management during administration. 

19. POWERS OF TRUSTEE. No hard and fast rule can be laid down relative to powers that should be conferred upon a trustee but the more important questions that must be answered may be summarized as follows: 

(a) What powers should be granted to the trustee in the matter of making investments? (b) By what formula, if any, should income be distinguished from principal? (c) Should the trustee be empowered to make distribution in kind? (d) Should the trustee be empowered to borrow money and as security therefor, to pledge and encumber property? (e) What principal encroachment powers should be reserved in favor of the trustee or some other person? (f) What powers should be granted to the trustee for the management of real property, especially to sell, partition and sub-divide? (g) Should the trustee be permitted to make leases extending beyond the termination of the trust and make improvements to real estate, the amortization of which cannot, in the ordinary course of events be made up during the life of the trust? 

20. APPOINTMENT OF EXECUTOR. The selection of an executor should be governed by the duties he will be required to perform. The testator in most instances, if left to his own devices, will suggest the appointment of his wife, a member of his family or some business associate. While it is entirely logical from the standpoint of economy to appoint some member of the family as executor if the testator's estate is not involved and the one to be appointed possesses good health, sound judgement and can give sufficient time to the task, it is the better practice to appoint a corporate fiduciary as executor if the testator's present estate is extensive or involved or if the family relationships are strained. 

Under certain conditions, and this is particularly true if the executor will be required to assume responsibility for the management of a close corporation or a partnership or the estate will consist of a large amount of unmarketable securities and unimproved realty to appoint a corporate fiduciary as executor and in order that the particular skill of testator's business associates or some member of his family may be [6]

availed of, to appoint: (a) Such person or persons as co-executor, or (b) Nominate such persons as estate advisors extending to them such exclusive or advisory powers as may seem most suitable to produce a harmonious relationship between the executor and the advisors and make the latter's talents helpful from the standpoint of administration. Ordinarily, the advisory method should be preferred as definite responsibility is then placed upon the corporate fiduciary to assume leadership in the administration of the estate and it permits the executor to handle detail matters that the law requires to be done but are not vital for the preservation of the estate, without taking the time of any co-executor or executors who are not administrative minded. The effectiveness of any plan of administration which is founded upon co-equal responsibility or places one person or a group in a position to control, check or supervise the accounts of another, depends upon their willingness to co-operate and the proper definement of their respective powers and duties. 

If the advisor's value to the estate lies along channels of business management, such powers as may be necessary for him to carry on any business of the testator should be reserved in his favor and to that extent control any general power granted to the executor. If the advisor is valuable to the estate as an investment or real estate counsellor, such definition of power should be made in his favor that will require the executor to advise with him in connection with all such matters. For forms suggesting the various ways in which executorships may be established under a will or trusts see 

21. APPOINTMENT OF TRUSTEE. Individuals do not by nature possess those qualifications necessary for ideal trust service. If a testator desires to appoint some individual as trustee for all or any part of his estate he should be made to understand that life is at best uncertain, that ill-health may overtake his trustee and render him incompetent, and that no matter how capable, if an individual be appointed the trust estate may at any time be suspended in mid-air, with no living and in some cases, no competent trustee to administer the trust except as the court may come to the rescue of the estate and appoint a successor. There may be good reasons from time to time why individuals should be appointed in a trust capacity, such as for instance, if no competent corporate trustee is available or where the performance of the trust requires for its effectiveness the peculiar genius of some particular individual. As a general rule, however, it is advisable to select a corporate fiduciary in any trust capacity. The arguments in favor of the appointment of a corporate fiduciary as opposed to that of an individual may be summarized as follows: 

ARGUMENTS FOR THE CORPORATE FIDUCIARY 

1. The corporate fiduciary makes it a business to administer estates and employs experienced and carefully trained executives to do the work. 

2. The corporate fiduciary does not depend upon the ability or integrity of any single individual; its services represent the combined efficiency of many. 

3. The corporate fiduciary, of necessity, has unquestioned financial responsibility. 

4. The organized fiduciary has an assured existence and permanency of location. 

5. The corporate fiduciary maintains a thorough system for the keeping of accounts and records and carefully protects the same. 

6. The organized fiduciary operates under provisions of the law and is subject to continuous supervision.

ARGUMENTS AGAINST INDIVIDUAL EXECUTOR OR TRUSTEE 

1. An individual usually acts in this capacity only once in a lifetime and in many cases does not have the necessary time to devote to administration. 

2. The individual usually acts alone, on his own responsibility and his knowledge of fiduciary matters is limited. 

3. Few individuals have the same degree of re sponsibility. 

4. The individual may change residence, take sick or die. 

5. Few individuals have the experience or patience to set up an adequate system for the proper administration of estates. 

6. The individual executor acts under no special supervision and his services depend upon health, experience, responsibility, available time and human frailties.

No law can compel an executor or trustee named under a will to accept such responsibility. It may be stated as a fact which requires no argument that the tremendous economic breakdown from which we are now suffering will result in greater discrimination on the part of corporate fiduciaries as to the types of trusts and executorships they will be willing to accept. If it seems logical to name a corporate fiduciary, take the proper officer or officers of such institution into your confidence, obtain his and their reaction to your client's plan; you may find their suggestions, based upon long experience in the administration of estates, really helpful and you will be able to determine in advance whether the fiduciary will be inclined to accept your client's trust or executorship when it comes time to administer his estate. [7]

FORM 7—ENTIRE ESTATE TO WIFE DECLARING REASON FOR EXCLUDING ISSUE 

All of the rest, residue and remainder of my estate that shall be capable of testamentary disposition by me, I give, devise and bequeath to my beloved wife, ----. --, absolutely and forever. (a) I have intentionally, and not as the result of any accident, mistake, or inadvertence, omitted in this, my will, to provide for any of my children, whether born before, or after, my death, or for the issue of a deceased child, whether born before, or after, my death. (b) In omitting to make provision for any such child, or issue of any such deceased child, and in vesting my residuary estate in my said wife,--- I have been prompted by the 

---

knowledge that she is fully conversant with all my plans for the future, touching each such child, or any issue of a deceased child, and I have utmost confidence in her ability and willingness to carry out such plans; and in making the declaration and explanation contained in this Article of my will, I do so out of deference to the statutes that require a testator expressly to declare his intention, in order to exclude from a will his children and the issue of any deceased child, and not with any desire, or intent, to detract, in any way, from the absolute character of the gift and devise to my wife of my residuary estate. 

Payment of Debts and Funeral Expenses FORM 8-PAYMENT OF DEBTS, LAST ILLNESS AND FUNERAL EXPENSES 

I direct that all my just debts, last illness and funeral expenses be paid by my executor as soon after my decease as may be found convenient.

FORM 9-DIRECTION TO PAY DEBTS INCLUDING BOTH LEGAL AS WELL AS THOSE UNENFORCIBLE 

I direct that all my debts, funeral and testamentary charges be paid as soon after my decease as may be found convenient, hereby giving to my executor full power and authority to recognize and pay as among such debts any promise or obligation made by me verbally or otherwise, which although not in such form that the holder thereof could compel payment by my estate, my executors think proper to be paid in their own judgment, or because from memoranda or verbal directions left by me or from other sources they are satisfied that it would be my wish to have paid.

Gifts of Household Goods and Personal Effects FORM 23-HOUSEHOLD GOODS AND PERSONAL EFFECTS 

I give and bequeath to my wife, to be her absolute property all of my personal effects, such as jewelry, household furniture and all other articles of domestic and personal use and any automobile or automobiles of mine that she may desire to retain and if she is not living at the time of my death, then to my issue or the survivors of them, share and share alike per stirpes and not per capita. 

FORM 24-FURNITURE AND HOUSEHOLD BELONGINGS 

I bequeath to my wife, all my furniture and personal belongings, the word furniture herein shall be construed to mean all my household possessions and all tapestries, pictures, engravings, paintings, portraits, plated and silverware, books, bric-a-brac, statuary and all works of art, domestic stores and all other portable articles in and about my residence at the time of my decease. 

FORM 25-BEQUEST OF PERSONAL EFFECTS TO WIFE; CONFIRMING OWNERSHIP OF HOME AND HOUSEHOLD GOODS IN HER 

I give and bequeath to my wife, my wearing apparel, watch and automobiles, and in case of her death before mine, I give and bequeath said articles to my son, The_ ---home, household furniture, pictures, plate, china, ornaments and all other household articles and family stores belong to my said wife, and I hereby confirm in her the title thereto. 1 [ 11 ]

FORM 26-PERSONAL EFFECTS IN RESIDENCE TO NIECES—EXECUTOR DIRECTED TO DEFRAY EXPENSES OF PACKING AND SHIPPING PERSONAL EFFECTS 

11119 

I give and bequeath to my three nieces, and___ all my personal effects, household furniture and books, mantel pieces, pictures, rugs and other items of household furniture and equipment in my residence in__. at the time of my death, to be divided equally between them, confident that they will agree upon an equitable division of the same into three lots, one for each, and I direct my Executor to defray the expense of packing and shipping or otherwise disposing of the same as my said nieces may direct. 

FORM 27-GIFT OF HOUSEHOLD GOODS ON CONDITION OF ASSUMPTION OF LEASE 

All of my household furniture, books, paintings, and all other articles that may be in, or used in, the apartment, or house, that I may occupy at the time of my death, I give and bequeath to my sister, upon condition, however, that she shall, by an instrument in writing delivered to my executors, within thirty (30) days after my death, assume and agree to perform all and singular the terms, provisions and conditions on my part to be performed of any subsisting lease covering the apartment, or house, that I may be a tenant of at the time of my death; and if my said sister, shall not thus assume and agree to perform any such lease, then, and in such event, this bequest to her shall be null and void, and all of the property herein mentioned shall fall into, and become part of, my residuary estate.

I bequeath to my wife, the sum of….

I give and bequeath to my said wife the sum of 

Dollars, payable immediately after my death, that she may be provided with funds in advance of the first payment of income as hereinafter provided.

FORM 31—ALLOWANCE UNTIL ACCRUAL AND PAYMENT OF INCOME 

I hereby direct that the beneficiaries under the trusts herein created shall be entitled to receive the income from the time of my death and in order to provide for them during the accrual and until the payment of income, I give and bequeath to them respectively, to be paid to them immediately after my death from my residuary estate, in addition to the income from said trust or trusts, a sum equal to---months of the fair average income therefrom as shall be estimated by my executors. 

FORM 32-INCOME OF ALL TRUSTS TO ACCRUE FROM DATE OF DEATH 

I direct that the income of all trusts and annuities created or given by this will shall be deemed to accrue for the benefit of the beneficiary or the annuitant from the date of my death; and pending the administration of my estate, and until the respective trust funds are established I direct my executors and trustees from time to time to pay from my general estate to the beneficiary of each trust such sum as in [12]

their judgment they shall determine to be equivalent to the income which the trust fund for the benefit of such beneficiary would have probably produced during such period had the same been invested, and to each annuitant the ratable amount of his or her annuity during such period. 

FORM 33-INCOME TO BE PAID FROM DEATH 

It is my intention and will that except where otherwise expressly provided, all gifts of income shall be adjusted and paid as of and from the date of my death. 

Simultaneous Death Provisions FORM 34-PROVISION IN EVENT OF CONCURRENT DEATH 

In case my wife, shall not survive me, or in the event that it shall be impossible to determine which of us died first, I dispose of my estate as follows: 

FORM 35-GIFT IN CASE OF COMMON DISASTER

FORM 36-EVENT OF COMMON DISASTER 

In the event that I predecease my wife and (a) I survive my son, or (b) my son and I both perish in a common disaster so that there is no presumption of survivorship between us, then, and in either case, I bequeath all my property to my wife.

In the event that I predecease my son, and (a) I survive my wife or (b) my wife and I both perish in a common disaster so that there is no presumption of survivorship between us, then and either case, I bequeath all my property to my son.

In the event that (a) I survive both my wife and my son or (b) all three of us perish in a common disaster so that there is no presumption of survivorship between us and either of them, then and in either case, I bequeath three-fourths of my property to my mother, brothers and sisters, living at the time of my death, share and share alike, and the remaining one-fourth thereof to----

FORM 38-DONEE SUCCUMBING IN SAME ACCIDENT, EPIDEMIC OR OTHER DISAS- TER TO BE REGARDED AS DYING BEFORE TESTATOR 

Should any beneficiary named herein die in the course of or as a direct result of the same accident, epidemic or other calamity as shall cause my death, then I give the bequest or devise allotted to him to such persons and in such manner and proportions as the same would have been taken under the terms of this will if such beneficiary had died before me. [13]

FORM 39-PROVISION THAT IF WIFE AND TESTATOR DIED IN COMMON DIS- ASTER, WIFE PREDECEASED TESTATOR IF TESTATOR AND SON, TESTATOR DEEMED TO HAVE PREDECEASED SON 

In case my wife and I shall die in a common disaster, or under such circumstances that it cannot be readily determined which of us died first, it is my will that she shall be deemd not to have survived me, whether or not I actually predecease her. In case my son and I shall die in a common disaster, or under such circumstances that it cannot be readily determined which of us died first, it is my will that such son shall be deemed to have survived me whether or not I actually predecease him.

FORM 43-EXCLUSION OF ADOPTED CHILDREN 

Wherever I have used the words "issue" or "children" in this will, it is my intention to limit them to issue or children by birth and blood and to exclude children by adoption.

FORM 48-PROVISION THAT UPON DEATH OF ANY LEGATEE, LEGACY SHALL LAPSE 

If one of the legatees above named shall predecease me, the bequest to such deceased legatee shall lapse, and it shall become a part of my residuary estate, to be disposed of as hereinafter provided. 

FORM 49-LAPSED LEGACIES TO CHILDREN OF LEGATEE; IF NONE, TO RESIDIUM 

It is my will that in the event that any of the legatees or devisees named in this my will shall die before me leaving children him or her surviving the legacy to such deceased person be paid to his or her children in equal portions per stirpes and not per capita, and in default of issue I direct that such legacy fall into and become a part of my residuary estate to be disposed of as herein provided, except as herein otherwise directed.

FORM 50-LAPSE PREVENTED BY GIFT OVER 

In the case of each and every bequest, and of every instance in which I have directed my Trustees to pay over money to any person or persons whomsoever, if the person or persons to whom and for whose benefit I have made such bequest or have directed money to be paid as aforesaid, shall have deceased, or from any cause shall be incapable of taking, then the amount so bequeathed or so directed to be paid over shall revert to and become a part of my residuary estate unless I have otherwise specifically directed.

FORM 57-AS TO INEQUALITIES IN GIFTS (Another Form) 

I have borne

In making the foregoing gift, bequest and devise to my said daughter, in mind not only the equal affection that exists between myself and all of my children, but, also, the fact that my other two children have heretofore been more amply provided for than my said daughter, FORM 58-AS TO INEQUALITIES IN GIFTS 

In reference to all the said gifts and appointments as to my daughters and to my son, declare that the inequalities between the property appropriated to them and that to my sons, and___ are not owing to any difference in affection, respect, esteem and regard for them, but inasmuch as they all receive very ample fortunes, I have intended to relieve them from the care and exactions which a larger fortune would carry with it. 

FORM 59-NO GIFT FOR WIFE BECAUSE OTHERWISE PROVIDED FOR 

I have already made substantial gifts unto my beloved wife, and have provided amply for her requirements, and for this reason, and with her full consent, I make no provision for her in this my last will and testament.

FORM 60—WHY NO CHARITABLE GIFTS—WIFE AND CHILDREN WILL PROVIDE 

Acting from a judgment deliberately formed, based upon observation of the expediency of testamentary bequests to religious and charitable objects, and believing it better and wiser to give liberally during my life [16]

to such objects, I make no bequest of that character. Knowing the hearty sympathy of my beloved wife in all that is good, I feel sure that should she survive me she will use the property entrusted to her hands, as a faithful steward of God, and I trust that by the guiding of a kind Providence my children and grandchildren have been so educated as to feel it a privilege and joy to give liberally and largely in proportion to their means for the advancement of the cause of our Blessed Redeemer, and to all humane and benevolent objects.

FORM 61-WHY NO CHARITABLE GIFTS-GIFTS HAVE BEEN MADE IN LIFETIME 

In this will I have made no bequests to religious, charitable or educational institutions or to individuals outside of my immediate family, for the reason that heretofore, at different times and in various ways, I have made donations, gifts and provisions which seem to me liberal in view of the limits of my fortune. 

If later it seems to me appropriate to increase the amounts of gifts already made or to make other gifts or provisions, I will take such action during my lifetime.

FORM 67-DIRECTION AUTHORIZING POSTPONEMENT OF PAYMENT OF BEQUESTS AND CREATION OF TRUSTS FOR TEN YEARS, WITH INTEREST AT RATE 

OF 32%

While it is my desire that the bequests above made shall be paid, and the trust funds above created shall be set apart, as soon as it may be convenient so to do, in the course of the administration of my estate, nevertheless, in view of the fact that the obligations of my estate under the terms of the co-partnership agreements hereinafter referred to, and otherwise, may be such that the amount thereof cannot definitely be [17]

determined until after the lapse of a considerable period of time, and in view of the further fact that my interests under the terms of the said co-partnership agreements may be of such character that the same cannot be readily realized upon, or liquidated, without sacrifice and loss to my estate, I direct that my executrix, or my executor, or executors, as the case may be, shall not be compelled to make payment of the said bequests, or payments on account thereof, or to set up in whole, or in part, the said trust funds, until after the lapse of the period of ten (10) years from the date of the issuance of letters testamentary upon my estate. With respect to interest, I direct that no interest shall accrue, or be payable, upon any of said bequests, or trust funds, until after the lapse of the period of ten (10) years from the date of the issuance of letters testamentary upon this will, except only that, beginning one year from the date on which letters testamentary are issued under this will, yearly interest of three and one-half (32%) per cent, shall be paid quarterly upon 'any unpaid portions of the legacies under Article__ hereof, and upon any undelivered portions of said trust funds under Article-. _hereof, such interest to be in lieu of any interest, or income, upon such unpaid, or undelivered portions which any legatee, or beneficiary, might otherwise be entitled to, during the period ending ten (10) years from the issuance of said letters testamentary.

FORM 68-PROVISION TO PREVENT FORCED SALE OF ASSETS, AND POSTPONING PAYMENT OF LEGACIES 

Whereas, so large a percentage of my property is vested in my own business and unmarketable stocks and bonds that it will require some time to convert my entire property into cash, with any reasonable conservation of said property; and

Whereas, to dispose of them to advantage means finding customers and favorable market conditions, and this may require years; and 

Whereas, I should need as much time to turn them into cash myself, and to expect my executors to accomplish this in less time than I should require, would be asking of them something they would doubtless be unable to perform;

Now therefore, I direct that my executors shall have -years, in which to convert my property into cash, and more time if, in the discretion of the court, they should have it, as forced liquidation of my business and other interests in which good-will is a big factor, would mean slaughter of my assets; and 

I direct that all bequests are to be paid as soon as sufficient money is realized from my estate to do so; and in the meantime, the legatees shall receive interest quarterly at the rate of---- -per cent per annum from the date of my death from the income of my estate on the amounts of their respective legacies; but I declare that it is my intention that this direction shall, in no way, prevent the vesting of said legacies immediately upon my death. Should, however, this direction conflict with the law against perpetuities, or otherwise, so as to render this, my will, or some part thereof inoperative, or invalid, then this direction is to be disregarded.

FORM 90-LEGACIES NOT SUBJECT TO ADEMPTION 

In making the above legacies in the form of trusts for the benefit of my said wife and sons and the wife of my said son, -, as well as making the disposition aforesaid for the benefit of my daughter, ----, my intention is to bequeath for their benefit severally and respectively sums of money equal to the value of the securities mentioned in the several Schedules, as the values are therein severally stated to be, and I do not intend that said legacies should be special, but general, so that the same shall not be subject to ademption, and so that they shall be subject to abatement in the same manner, but not otherwise, as they would be were they general legacies of money simply, my purpose in mentioning and describing the securities being that I now have them in my possession and approve the same as a permanent investment of these legacies. If, however, upon my death there be not among my assets all the securities thus mentioned and described in the Schedules, I direct so many as I may then have to be assigned proportionately to the said several legacies, according to the several and respective amounts of such legacies, and that the remainder of the money values stated in the several legacies be made up and completed by the transfer and assignment of other securities left by me or purchased and approved by them, my said executors, or by the payment of money, and for the purpose of completing the amounts of the said several legacies, in case of any deficiency of bonds and stocks such as are mentioned in the said several Schedules, I authorize my said executors to make investments on bond and mortgage or to purchase and pay for out of the assets of my estate other bonds or securities sufficient to make up such deficiency; but in case of any such investment upon bond and mortgage, I direct that the mortgages shall be upon real estate lying within the City of____ and in case of investments by the purchase of bonds or other securities, I authorize my executors to make such investments in obligations pledged by the full faith and credit of the United States or one of the several States of the Union. [ 21 ]

Abatement FORM 91-ABATEMENT OF LEGACIES IN FAVOR OF PARTICULAR ITEM 

No part of the property which is bequeathed and devised under this item of my will shall be taken for any debt or other claim against my estate unless all my other property, real, personal and mixed, shall have first been exhausted.

FORM 92—ABATEMENT OF LEGACIES 

(a) If my estate shall prove insufficient to pay all the legacies and bequests given by this will, then it is my direction that the bequests to my wife, shall first be paid in full and that the other legacies shall abate proportionately. 

(b) If from losses or depreciation my estate should prove insufficient to pay all legacies and devises in full, then I direct that the pecuniary and specific legacies shall abate proportionately, but there shall be no resort to or abatement in any specific devise in order to make up any deficiency in any specific or general bequest of personal property. (c) I direct that in case of a deficiency of assets for the full payment of all the pecuniary legacies hereinbefore bequeathed, the two legacies of-- 

__Dollars and___. 

Dollars, hereinbefore bequeathed to the said legatees respectively, shall abate ratably and in proportion before any other legacy. (d) I direct that if the moneys to arise from my estate applicable to the payment of debts and legacies shall not be sufficient to pay in full such debts and the legacies thereby bequeathed, then the legacies hereby given to--- _and__ _shall abate proportionately in favor of the other legacies hereby given.

FORM 93-DIRECTION THAT LEGACIES BE PAID IN ORDER IN WHICH NAMED 

I hereby direct that the bequests made by the foregoing provisions of this my will shall be paid as near as practicable in the order in which they are above named, and that each of said bequests shall be paid as soon as payments can conveniently be made from the net income from my said estate, or by the application of any bonds or railroad stocks to my said estate belonging, at a proper valuation, or from moneys in the hands of my said executors applicable thereto.

FORM 98-LOSSES BY TESTATOR AFTER MAKING WILL CHARGED AGAINST CHAR- ITABLE BEQUESTS 

Whereas since the execution of my last will and testament I have made considerable investments in real and leasehold properties and in stocks and bonds the value of which are now greatly depreciated and I have suffered losses of large sums of money in my various business transactions and whereas I am determined that the legacies and bequests to my wife and the issue of my body named in my said last will and testament shall not be reduced because of said investments and losses or because of other investments and losses to be by me made or incurred, I do expressly charge any sum in which my estate may be deficient for the payment of said legacies and bequests ratably and proportionately upon the devises, legacies and bequests to charitable institutions and for charitable purposes appearing in said last will and testament.

FORM 127-SPECIAL POWERS OF EXECUTOR AND TRUSTEE TO CONTINUE BUSI- NESS OF TESTATOR 

(a) I hereby expressly authorize my said executor, if he (or it) shall so elect, to continue and operate, during the probate administration of my estate, any business operation or enterprise in which I am inter- [ 28 ]

ested at the time of my death, all profits and losses therefrom belonging to and falling upon my estate and not upon my said executor personally. FORM 128-POWER TO EXECUTOR AND TRUSTEE TO CONTINUE PARTNERSHIP TO CONFORM TO TERMS OF PARTNERSHIP AGREEMENT

This provision shall not, however, be taken as charging such executor or trustee of my estate beyond the terms of such partnership agreement, nor as in any event requiring such executor or trustee to any extent to incur any personal obligation, but the rights of all devisees and legatees hereunder shall be subject to the above provisions hereof.

FORM 132—EXECUTOR AND TRUSTEE AUTHORIZED TO ENTER INTO CONTRACT WITH SURVIVING PARTNERS FOR OPERATION OF BUSINESS The_ 

as Executor and Trustee hereunder, is hereby expressly authorized to enter into contract with such surviving partners, of the following general tenor:

First: In no event shall such Executor or Trustee become a partner with such surviving partners, but either it or such person as it shall nominate to represent it, shall become a sub-partner of one or more, but not of all of such surviving partners. 

Second: The interest of my estate in the capital, good will and business of such co-partnership may be assigned and transferred to one or more, but not all of such surviving partners, upon agreement and consideration that said transferees shall together with the other surviving partners constitute themselves a new partnership to which all of the assets of the old partnership and its good will, and all right to use names shall be contributed, including all interest so transferred by said estate, such new partnership to be formed under an agreement, the terms of which shall be approved by the trustee hereunder, and to continue for a period of not more than_. ----years. Under said new partnership agreement the transferees of said interest of my estate in said old partnership, shall receive and be entitled to an interest in both profits and assets, at least equal to the proportion of such interest of said estate to all of the other capital, provided personal services may be compensated by salaries as the Trustee may approve, but except as agreed upon, salaries shall not be payable in excess of the stipulations therefor. 

Said transferees of said interest of said estate in said old partnership shall covenant and agree to hold, account for in trust, and shall be held to have assigned and transferred to the Trustee or its nominee hereunder, but only as a sub-partner, such interest in the interest of said transferees in said new firm, both in income, profits and assets distributable to said transferees as partners in said new firm, as said Trustee shall require which shall not be less than such an interest in the entire income and assets of said new partnership, as the proportion at the time of my death of the interest of my estate in all of the then property of said partnership, inclusive of a reasonable valuation for good will and use of name. 

FORM 133-EXECUTOR AND TRUSTEE TO CONTINUE BUSINESS FOR LIMITED TIME, EMPLOY ESTATE'S CAPITAL, AND FORM CORPORATION 

I hereby expressly authorize and empower my executor and Trustee hereunder, respectively and successively to continue to carry on the business I am engaged in at the time of my death, and from time to time in such way as may be advisable to use, pledge or employ in and about the conduct of such business not to exceed fifty (50) per cent of the amount of capital by me employed therein at the time of my death. 

Said Executor and Trustee is authorized to continue such business for such part of seven (7) years after my death as it deems best, and at such time as it deems best within said period to liquidate the same. 

The Executor and/or Trustee hereunder is, however, expressly authorized to cause to be organized a corporation for the operation of such business and to transfer to such corporation the assets of the estate so authorized to be employed in such business for all of the stock of such corporation other than shares thereof requisite to be issued to qualify officers and directors of such corporation. 

FORM 134-TESTATOR'S BUSINESS-EXECUTATORS AND TRUSTEES TO CONTINUE BUSINESS—CHILDREN TO TAKE OVER BUSINESS AS EACH BECOMES 21PROVISION FOR TERMINATION OF BUSINESS IF UNPROFITABLE 

It is my earnest wish that my children upon attaining their majorities may be able to earn a livelihood, I therefore give and bequeath to my Executors and Trustees, hereinafter named, for the benefit of my said children, in equal shares, the business now owned and conducted by me under the name of- and style of___ 

---, and I direct that said business shall be continued after my death by my said Executors and Trustees for the benefit of my said children, during their respective minorities. As each one [ 30 ]

of my children attains the age of twenty-one (21) years, the share of said child in said business shall be turned over to such child and in the case of the death of any such children before attaining the age of twentyone (21) years and leaving issue surviving, such share shall be turned over to the issue of such deceased child. So much of the income from said business as in the opinion of the Executors and Trustees shall be sufficient to keep my children in comfort, shall be paid over to my wife, for the support, maintenance and education of my said children, during their respective minorities. Such payments shall be made semi-annually.

In the event, however, at the end of any year it is found that the said business does not yield a profit, and in the opinion of my Executors and Trustees, the said business will not yield a profit if continued, then I direct my Executors and Trustees to wind up and dispose of the said business, and the proceeds so realized therefrom shall be added to my residuary estate for the benefit of my children as hereinafter provided, and such proceeds shall become a part of such residuary estate. 

FORM 135-EXECUTOR TO CONTINUE BUSINESS WITH SURVIVING PARTNERS; 

POWER TO SELL INTEREST TO SURVIVING PARTNERS 

If at my death I shall be a member of any co-partnership, by the articles of co-partnership of which it shall be provided that such co-partnership shall be continued after my decease, I hereby specially and fully authorize and direct that my executors shall in all and every necessary and useful manner and respect, cooperate with the survivors of such co-partnership in continuing and carrying on the business thereof until the full end and term provided by such articles agreeably to the provisions and to the terms specified and contained therein; and I specially authorize and empower my executors either before or after the expiration of such co-partnership to sell to my surviving partners either for cash or upon credit, and such price as to them shall seem suitable and satisfactory, all my interest in such co-partnership, and in the assets, business, property and profits thereof; and I further specially direct and authorize either of my executors who shall be a member of such co-partnership to co-operate in such sale notwithstanding the fact.

All the rest, residue and remainder of the real and personal property, which at the time of my death shall belong to me or be subject to my disposal by will, including lapsed legacies and devises, I give, devise and bequeath to my wife, if living; and, if not, to my issue per stirpes.

FORM 158-ENTIRE RESIDUE TO WIFE 

All the rest, residue and remainder of my estate, both real and personal, of whatsoever kind and nature, and wheresoever the same may be situated, of which I shall die seized or possessed, or in which I may in any wise be entitled at the time of my decease, I give, devise and bequeath unto my wife, to have and to hold the same unto her, her heirs, legal representatives and assigns, absolutely and forever. 

FORM 159-TO BROTHERS AND SISTERS; AND CHILDREN OF ANY THAT ARE DEAD -ONE NEPHEW DISINHERITED 

I give, bequeath and devise all the rest of and residue of my estate, both real and personal or mixed, that may remain after the payment of the items hereinbefore named, and the costs and expenses of administration, to my brothers and sisters who may be living at my death and the children of any who may be dead, with the exception of my nephew, who is to have no share, to be equally divided between them, except that the children of a deceased brother or sister shall take per stirpes in such division, that is they shall stand in the place of their deceased parent or take only the share that their parent should have taken had he or she been living at my death.

Dower Provisions FORM 162-PROVISIONS FOR WIFE IN LIEU OF DOWER 

(a) The provision made in this will for my wife is in lieu of dower, distributive share, years support, and any other interest she might, under the law, have in my estate if I left no will. (b) And I do hereby declare that the foregoing provision in favor of my wife is intended to be in lieu of any and all rights of dower, distributive share, years allowance or other interest or right in my estate. In the event my wife shall elect not to take under this will then I direct upon such happening that the executor herein shall pay over and distribute to her such portion of my estate as she may be entitled to receive under the law and the remainder of said estate shall be held and distributed in the manner at times and under the terms and conditions as herein set forth. 

FORM 163-PROVISIONS FOR WIFE IN LIEU OF DOWER WITHOUT DEPRIVING HER OF RIGHT OF QUARANTINE 

The provisions contained in this will, in favor of my said wife, are made, and shall be accepted by her, in lieu of, and in place of, dower and of all her right of dower; and if she shall accept, or elect to take, her dower and her right of dower, or either of them, then the provisions for her, which are contained in this will, shall be of no force and effect whatsoever and as if not contained herein. But nothing herein contained shall be deemed, or construed, to deprive my said wife, of any right of quarantine, which may be given to her, or be vested in her, as my widow, under any law of the 

State of that may be in force, at the time of my death.

FORM 164 PROVISIONS FOR WIFE IN LIEU OF DOWER WHERE PROPERTY OF TESTATOR IS IN SEVERAL STATES 

The provisions of this will in favor of my wife are intended to be in lieu of dower and all other provisions for the widow made by the laws of this State or any other of the several states where any of my estate, real or personal may be situated, as well as in lieu of any claim she might otherwise have by reason of any law relating to the community of property of a husband and wife on account of, on or against any property or estate owned, held or controlled by me or standing in my name at the time of my death, or at any time prior thereto. And in case my said wife shall not accept such provisions as intended by me for her benefit, I direct that upon her failure to accept as aforesaid, her share or interest in the income and principal designed for her benefit shall cease and be disposed of in the same manner as it would have been in case of her death prior to my own.

FORM 165-GIFT IN ADDITION TO DOWER, ETC. 

(After gift to wife, either directly or in trust), all the provisions heretofore made in this will for my wife, are intended to be in addition to her dower interest in my realty, and her distributive share of my personalty, and her claim and allowance out of my estate for support, and any or all other rights, interests, or claims, which are given to her, against my estate, by the law. 

Miscellaneous Provisions FORM 166-USE OF HOMESTEAD BY WIFE 

The trustee shall allow my beloved wife, during her life, the free use and enjoyment of any real estate used by me as a homestead at the time of my death, and during that time she shall not be required to see to the payment of taxes or maintenance of insurance thereon, unless funds of the trust in the possession of the trustee are insufficient to pay the same.

FORM 168-CORPORATE FIDUCIARY TO HAVE CUSTODY 

I hereby direct that the corporate executor hereof and trustee hereunder shall be and it is hereby charged with the custody and safe keeping of all money and securities belonging to my estate and that upon the same coming into the custody of the corporate executor or trustee the individual executors hereof and trustees hereunder shall be and they are hereby relieved of responsibility as to the custody and safe keeping of the same.

FORM 169-PROTECTING HYPOTHECATED SECURITIES 

I authorize and empower my executors hereinafter named in their discretion, to protect any and all investments and purchases of stock, bonds or other securities which I have made during my life, and which are hypothecated or not fully paid for at the time of my death, and to protect any and all accounts which are not closed at such time, and for such purpose or purposes to make advances to my estate and to borrow money upon and to hypothecate any securities belonging to my estate without obligating themselves personally for the repayment of the same.

FORM 170-FIDUCIARIES MAY PURCHASE ASSETS 

I authorize and empower any of my executors or trustees to purchase any portion of my estate, whether real or personal, notwithstanding the fact of such executorship or trusteeship, and for the purpose of such purchase I authorize and direct my other executors or trustees to fix the price in their absolute discretion and the same shall be binding, for all purposes, and the deed of assignment to such purchaser by my other executors or trustees shall give complete and perfect title in the same manner as though such purchaser had not been nominated or appointed by me as such executor or trustee and had not acted as such. 

FORM 171-BENEFICIARY MAY PURCHASE ASSETS 

Any beneficiary of the trusts hereby created shall have the right to purchase any of the trust property and otherwise to deal with the trustee as freely as strangers to the trust. 

FORM 172-RELATIVES AFFORDED OPPORTUNITY TO TAKE AT APPRAISED VAL- UATION 

I will and direct however that my executors, before making public sale of any of the contents of said house, shall give a reasonable opportunity to any of my relatives or to any of the relatives of my dear husband to purchase any of said articles at a price satisfactory to my said executors, and not less than the appraised valuation thereof. I also will and direct that my diamonds, watches, chains and all other jewelry not herein specifically bequeathed shall also be sold by my said executors, but my relatives and the relatives of my dear husband, shall be given a reasonable opportunity to purchase any of such jewelry, in the manner as hereinbefore provided for the contents of my said residence. 

Disinheritance Clauses

FORM 173-CONDITIONS AGAINST CONTEST OF WILL 

may be

I anticipate no obstruction to the amicable administration of my estate; nevertheless, I think it prudent to stipulate, this condition, to-wit: that any person, named beneficiary herein, who directly or indirectly resists the probate or contests the validity, hereof, shall by such opposition or contest be thereby ab initio barred and divested of the benefits hereby provided for him or her under my will and his or her distributive share shall then become part of my residuary estate and be distributed as part thereof. 

FORM 174-DISPUTING WILL 

I do further direct and declare that each and every gift, legacy or provision of this my will whereby any money or property is given to or for the benefit of any person or persons is subject to the following express condition, that is to say, that the person or persons to whom or, in case of trust, for whose benefit [ 37 ]

such money or property is given, shall not in any manner contest or oppose the probate of this my will (which shall include any codicil hereto) or assert in any manner direct or indirect, before any judicial tribunal that the same is not my last will and testament (if testator desires to go so far, add: or deny to call in question before any judicial tribunal the legal validity of any of the dispositions or provisions therein contained), and if requested so to do, by or on behalf of one or more of the persons named as my executors or other proponent of the will, shall join in the application for or assent to the probate of this my last will and testament and in case any such person or persons fail to thus give his consent or shall otherwise violate such condition, then in such case and in every such case the money or property which is by any provision of this will destined to or for the benefit of such person or persons and any estate, share or interest in any property real or personal left by me at my death or to which they or any of them might be or become entitled as my heirs, next of kin or otherwise shall thereupon go; and I give, devise and bequeath the same to such of the beneficiaries entitled to take under this will as shall not have violated the condition aforesaid, in the same share and in the same manner as if such person or persons so offending against the provisions of this paragraph at the time of so offending had died without descendants. 

FORM 175-DISCOURAGING LITIGATION 

I direct that, in case any litigation whatever rises in respect to this will, or any of its provisions, or its probate, or concerning the trusts hereby created, or any of them, all of the costs, disbursements, and allowances awarded by the court to all the parties concerned in such litigation, and also all the costs, disbursements, counsel fees, and expenses of every name, nature and description, which may be incurred by the executors of, or the trustees under this will, in defending or preparing to defend any such legal proceedings, or in taking advice with respect to their right and duties concerning the same, or with respect to the construction of this will, or any questions growing out thereof, shall be charged against the amount coming under this will or otherwise from my estate to the persons commencing or joining in such legal proceedings against the said executors or trustees and shall be considered as so much money paid to such persons or applied to their use, with like effect in every respect as if the same amount of money, or its equivalent, had been paid, transferred, or conveyed directly to them by my said executors or trustees as aforesaid shall be accepted as conclusive upon any accounting by them with respect thereto, both as to the reasonableness of any counsel fees paid and as to the correctness of all payments made. 

FORM 176-PROVISION AGAINST CONTEST 

If any legatee or devisee shall directly or indirectly contest or dispute the probate of this will or maintain before any judicial body that this is not my last will or call in question before any tribunal the provisions of any legacy, devise or provision herein, then I absolutely revoke the legacy, devise or provision to said persons and declare the same void and of no effect and I give said legacy, devise or provision so revoked to_ 

FORM 177—BENEFICIAL INTEREST VOID IF WILL CONTESTED 

In the event that any beneficiary under this, my last will and testament, shall attempt, either directly or indirectly, to set aside, or contest, or cause to be contested, or shall aid, in any manner, in the contesting of this, my last will and testament, or any part thereof, or any provision therein contained, or the probate thereof, or make any claim against me, or my estate, other than to claim the benefits therein provided for, for such beneficiary, then, and in that event, the devise, bequest, or other provision for said beneficiary's benefit herein contained, shall, thereupon, lapse and cease, with the same force and effect as if said devise, bequest, or other provision, for said beneficiary's benefit were not contained in this will; and I further direct that the said legacy, bequest, or other benefit, herein provided for, for said beneficiary, shall go and fall into my residuary estate, to be disposed of as part of my residuary estate, as herein provided. 

FORM 178—DISINHERITANCE CLAUSE 

I have intentionally omitted all of my children, and all surviving children of any deceased child and all other heirs of mine who are not specifically mentioned herein, and I hereby generally and specifically disinherit each, any and all persons whosoever claiming to be, or who may be lawfully determined to be my heirs, except only as mentioned and provided for in this will. 

FORM 179-DISINHERITANCE FOR DISOBEDIENCE 

I give and bequeath to my son, the sum of one ($1) dollar; and I hereby declare that it is my will that this provision for him shall be in full satisfaction of every right and interest in and to my estate, or to any part thereof. I make only this provision for him, for reasons well known to him and to [ 38 ]

other members of my family, and, in particular, for the reason that he has been both disobedient and ungrateful to me for many years prior to the date of the making of this will. 

FORM 180-COSTS OF LITIGATION TO BE CHARGED AGAINST LITIGATING LEGA- 

TEES I direct that in case any litigation whatever arises in respect to this Will or any of its provisions or its probate or concerning the trusts hereby created, or any of them, all the costs, disbursements and allowances awarded by the Court to all the parties concerned in such litigation, and also all the costs, disbursements, counsel fees and expenses of every name, nature and description, which may be incurred by the Executors of or the Trustees under this Will in defending or preparing to defend any such legal proceedings, or in taking advice with respect to their rights and duties concerning the same, or with respect to the construction of this Will or any question growing out thereof, shall be charged against (here set forth legacies, devises and/or bequests which shall be chargeable with such expense). 

FORM 1802-MARRIAGE WITHOUT CONSENT 

I hereby declare and provide that if any of my children shall marry without my consent during my lifetime, or thereafter without the consent of a majority of the then executors and trustees under this will, then and in that event the share allotted to the child so marrying in and by said will, shall be reduced onehalf, and the principal of the other half of the said share shall be paid, assigned, transferred or set over to such persons as under the laws of the State of___ -would take the same if I had died intestate.

Provisions Relating to Charity FORM 202-DONATIONS TO CHARITY 

Until the division of my estate, my executors and trustees are authorized and empowered to make such reasonable donations therefrom to charitable or benevolent purposes as in their judgment I would have made if living; all such donations to be charged against the whole of said trust estate and to that extent in reduction thereof at the division of the same. All such donations I leave entirely to the judgment and discretion of said executors and trustees, as they are fully advised of my purposes and inclinations in that respect. 

FORM 203-PAYMENT OF CHARITABLE SUBSCRIPTIONS 

I authorize and direct my executors to pay any subscriptions or amounts which I have agreed to pay for charitable, philanthropic, religious or other purposes, in accordance with my promise or subscription. If at the time of my death I shall be engaged in the construction of any building for charitable, philanthropic, educational or religious purposes, I direct my executors to complete the same at the expense of my estate, following as far as shall seem suitable to them in their discretion the plans if any existing therefor. I authorize my executors in their discretion to change such plans, and, so far as there shall not be plans for any such building, I direct my executors to provide the same in their discretion.

FORM 209—CHARITABLE GIFTS TO GO TO WIFE IF STATUTE MAKES SAME VOID 

Should I die leaving my wife, me surviving, and should my death occur within. months after the execution of this will, so as to make my charitable bequests ineffectual or void according to law, or should any of the gifts contained therein be ineffective or invalid in any degree by reason of my having given more than one-half of my estate to benevolent, charitable, literary, scientific, religious or missionary societies, associations or corporations, or otherwise, I hereby give, devise and bequeath to my said wife, absolutely and without condition, all my estate, real and personal, wheresoever and whatsoever, which by reason of my dying within * --after the execution of this will, or by reason of the law limiting such testamentary gifts to one-half of a testator's estate, or which for any other reason may not be sufficiently disposed of by my said will. 

FORM 210-PARTIAL INVALIDITY 

Should any of the provisions or directions of this will fail, or be held ineffectual or invalid for any reason, it is my will that no other portion or provision of this will be invalidated, impaired or affected thereby, but that this will be construed as if such invalid provsion or direction had not been herein contained.

maintain and equip laboratories, clinics, dispensaries and other places for such study and research and to provide for the proper housing of the same; to publish and disseminate the results of such study and research not only in scientific journals and for physicians and scientists, but also, and this I do especially enjoin on said legatees, by popular publications, public lectures and other appropriate methods to give to the people of the United States generally the benefits of increased knowledge concerning the prevention of sickness and disease and also concerning the conservation of health by proper food and diet.

FORM 229-LECTURESHIP IN RELIGION IN LIGHT OF SCIENCE AND PHILOSOPHY 

The object of this foundation is not the promotion of scientific investigation and discovery, but rather the assimilation and interpretation of that which has been or shall be hereafter discovered, and its application to human welfare, especially by the building of the truths of science and philosophy into the structure of a broadened and purified religion. to this end it is desired that lectures or a series of lectures be given by men eminent in their respective departments, on ethics, the history of civilization and religion, biblical research, all sciences and branches of knowledge which have an important bearing on the subject, all the great laws of nature, especially of evolution.. -; also such interpretations of literature and sociology as are in accord with the spirit of this Foundation to the end that the Christian spirit may be nurtured in the fullest light of the world's knowledge and that mankind may be helped to attain its highest possible welfare and happiness upon this earth.

It is desired that a liberal portion of the money available for the object of this Foundation shall be expended in the publication and dissemination of such lectures, and other utterances or literature orignating outside this Foundation, which are in line with the objects herein specified, and for the condensation or abbreviation of a part of such material making it more likely to be read by business men and the people in general and to be printed in the public press, and thus promote greater intelligence in religious life, purer ideals in public worship, and religious spirit that is in harmony with the view of the world held by science and philosophy.

FORM 230-PUBLICATION OF BOOK APPROVED BY THE UNIVERSITY COMMITTEE ON PUBLICATIONS 

It is my wish that the annual income of this gift, or of its accumulations for periods of not to exceed four years each, shall be used to aid in the publication of some book approved by the University Committee on Publications. Knowing, however, that the present working capital of the Press is entirely inadequate for its needs, I am willing that an amount not to exceed three-fourths (34) of the fund be advanced to the Press, whenever the____ Committee specifically approves, to meet the expense of publishing some important work from the proceeds of which the fund is to be reimbursed. 

It would be a source of added pleasure to me if this small gift, made in grateful memory of my father, should be used at least occasionally-to aid publications in the field of New England History, in which he was specially interested. [ 47 ]

I give, bequeath and devise all the rest and residue of my estate, whether real or personal and wherever situated, absolutely and in fee, to the President and Fellows of_____ ---College in. the principal thereof to be permanently held and invested by them and one-half the income thereon to be used for the purchase of books for the University Library of the Class usually described as Belles Lettres, and the remaining one-half to be used either for the purchase of other books for the said library or for any of the expenses connected with its administration. 

FORM 233-COLLECTION OF BOOKS, MANUSCRIPTS, AND LETTERS OF AMERICAN AUTHORS, TO BE HELD SEPARATELY 

._to___ 

University a collection of first and notable editions, manuscripts and letters of American Authors. It is to be held by the University under the following conditions: (1) The collection is to be kept apart by itself always in a separate room in the University Library Building, which room is to be furnished with chairs, tables and all reasonable accommodations for study. 

Any books of similar character, however, which may be given to the University by any other person, or any books of like nature now or hereafter in the University Library, may be placed in the same room and added to this collection, at the discretion of the Librarian, and the provisions herein contained then apply to such added books. (2) A special custodian shall be appointed by the Librarian to take charge of these books and to be responsible for their condition and safe keeping, under the control of the Librarian. (3) No stamp or mark or writing of any kind shall be made in or upon any of these books, except in such pencil as can be erased. Book plates, however, may be pasted in them on the inside covers or end paper of the books, and leather labels with library reference numbers and other reasonable distinguishing marks printed thereon and in gilt, may be placed on the backs of any covers or cases, but not on the original cloth or leather bindings.

As I hope that this may prove to be the nucleus of a thorough and complete collection of all first and notable editions of American Authors of Belles Lettres down to the year 1911, and as I believe that others may be induced to add to it under the above conditions, I prefer not to have it called by my name, but to have it known as the -Collection of American Literature." Any title, however, agreeable to the 

University authorities will be satisfactory to me provided it does not contain my own name.

FORM 236-TYPE OF BUILDING AND MAINTENANCE OF LIBRARY; AIM AND OBJECT 

I desire the building to be tasteful, substantial and fireproof, and that a sufficient fund be reserved over and above the cost of its construction to provide, maintain and support a library for all time. I desire that the books and periodicals be selected with a view to create and sustain healthy moral and Christian sentiment in the community, and that all nastiness and immorality be excluded; I do not mean by this that there shall not be anything but hymn books and sermons, but I mean that dirty French novels and all skeptical trash of questionable moral tone shall never be found in this library. 

I want its atmosphere that of the Christian refinement and its aim and object the building up of character, and I rest content that the friends I have named will carry out my wishes in these particulars.

FORM 240-FOR STUDENT OF TWO OR MORE YEARS IN COLLEGE OF CERTAIN CHARACTERISTICS 

First: The income of said Fund shall be divided into two equal parts, one of which shall be awarded each year to a student in___ College who shall have two or more years to study before graduation, who shall receive such income during said two years, unless he shall be deprived of the same by the Committee, hereinafter named, for cause which they shall deem good and sufficient. 

Second: The student to whom such income shall be awarded shall be a man who is wholly, or in part, dependent upon his own efforts for his expenses in going through College, and who also possesses the following qualification: (1) Sound and strong character. (2) Marked ability. (3) High standing in the college world and in the estimation of his classmates. 

It is further desired that in judging a candidate's ability scholarship shall not be regarded as an indispensable factor, but only as one among various evidences; and that the sort of ability which promises continued development and future usefulness shall be chiefly sought. [ 49 ]

In general terms it may be said that it is the wish of the donors of this Fund that its benefits shall reach, so far as may be, a man of the character, ability, standing and circumstances of--- .___of the class of__. 

and to that end we accompany this gift with a brief biographical sketch of him, that it may be more apparent to successive Committees of award and to successive beneficiaries of the Fund what manner of man he was.

Third: The awarding of the income of this Fund shall be exclusively in the hands of a Committee consisting of the Dean of the academical department and one member of the Class of which member shall be chosen by the Secretary of the Class. When it shall be no longer possible for a member of the class of‒‒‒‒‒‒‒ to act upon this committee the Dean alone shall constitute such Committee. 111)

Fourth: Should the income of this Fund be awarded to a student receiving beneficiary aid or abatement from the College, it is provided that such income shall be in addition to such aid or abatement. 

Fifth: Should any recipient of the income of this Fund repay to the College the sum so received, or any part thereof, said sum so repaid shall be added to, and made a part of the principal sum of this Fund. 

FORM 241-FELLOWSHIP AS MEMORIAL INCOME TO USE OF PERSON SELECTED FOR MERIT, ABILITY AS A SCHOLAR, AND STUDY OF NON-PROFES- SIONAL SUBJECT 

The income to be applied in each year to the use of such graduate of said Department as may be selected annually by the Academic Faculty and on condition that he prosecute special studies not professional, to be prescribed by said Faculty, in some branch of literature or science, and reside at the college thirty-six weeks of the academic year; the same person to hold the Fellowship and receive the income of the Fund for not more than two successive years. The Faculty of the Academic Department shall have the right from time to time to change the subject of the studies in their discretion, but only one branch of study is to be assigned to any individual elected as a Fellow, and the same is not to be changed during the incumbency of the Fellowship. The said Faculty shall have the right to make and enforce such rules and regulations not inconsistent with the above, respecting such Fellowship and the incumbency thereof as they deem best.

FORM 242-AWARD UPON COMPETITIVE EXAMINATION 

The examination shall begin not more than three weeks after the Commencement of the third collegiate term. Any member of the Junior or Sophomore classes of the Academic Department may be a candidate. 

The examinations shall consist chiefly of printed papers to be answered in writing, the viva voce part shall not be more than one-eighth, and the classical papers shall include extracts from Homer, Cicero, Virgil, Horace, Catullus, and Lucretius with critical questions on the passages set, and translation from English prose into Latin prose; and may include extracts from other classical authors, questions in ancient History, and translations from English prose into Greek prose, at the option of the examiners, but no original composition in Latin or Greek. The mathematical papers shall always include three propositions of Euclid, taken from the first four books and the sixth book. 

The Scholarship shall be tenable till the regular time of taking the degree of M. A. (i. e., for three years after graduation as B. A.), one-third of the annual payment to be forfeited by non-residence in... 

The scholar shall not be deprived of his scholarship for any offense short of one involving expulsion, but if rusticated or suspended shall forfeit the proceeds during the time of his rustication or suspension. All forfeits shall be added to the principal fund sum. 

In case of equality, the examiners may divide the scholarship or hold a new examination between the equal candidates.

The examiners shall have power to fill accidental vacancies and the Corporation of--- 

College agree- 

1. To discharge the expenses of printing and stationery necessary to the examination. 

2. To distinguish the scholar in their annual catalogue by some appropriate mark.

With discretionary power in the Faculty, with the concurrence of the President to apply such income at any time they should think needful in aid of the salary of the professor of such language in the College. FORM 245-SEPARATE LOAN FUND FOR AID OF WORTHY STUDENTS; NAME OF DONOR; LOANS FROM INCOME 

66 this gift in the form of an addition to the "Loan Fund" recently established by the Corporation for the aid of students of the University, whose character and promise make them worthy of such help. I should be glad to have the fund bear the name of the --Loan Fund" and be separately administered in perpetuity for that purpose, each loan from the fund being so designated, if practicable.

While I do not wish to restrict loans absolutely to the income from this fund, I do wish that the principal should be kept intact, if possible, and with this object, I suggest that at least fifty per cent of all loans should be drawn from the income so that any encroachment upon the principal of the fund will be represented by at least 200 per cent in students' notes. This would admit of loans to amount of $----- -----per annum and the repayment of one-half of these loans would keep the principal intact. 

With these suggestions I am quite satisfied to leave the administration of the fund to the discretion of the Corporation, with permission moreover to supply the fund to other purposes, if at any time in the future the interests of the University should seem to require it.

I hereby direct my executors to sell my real property not herein specifically devised, at public or private sale, for cash or upon credit, at such time or times and on such terms as shall seem to them best, and I direct that said real estate, for the purposes of this will, shall be deemed converted into personalty as of the time of my death.

FORM 249-EQUITABLE CONVERSION-ANOTHER FORM

FORM 250-EQUITABLE CONVERSION WITH DISCRETION ON EXECUTORS AS TO TIME OF SALE

I leave to the discretion of the said executors of this my will or of such of them as shall act, and the survivors and survivor of them the choice of the particular time for making sales from time to time of the said real estate.

FORM 253—DEATH TAXES TO BE BORNE PROPORTIONATELY BY TRUSTS 

In the event that any death taxes, however designated, shall be imposed wholly or partly in respect to the property of this trust the Trustee shall pay the same or its proportionate part thereof when due or at its option may make payment of its proportionate part to the person against whom assessed upon his assuming the liability for the entire tax. If any such tax shall be imposed partly in respect to the property of this trust and partly in respect to other property the Trustee shall pay a proportion of the total tax equivalent to the proportion which the property of this trust on which such tax is imposed bears to the total property on which it is imposed, giving such weight to exemptions, deductions and other factors and paying also such part of any interest or penalty due on such a tax as shall appear in its judgment to be reasonable and just or as it shall in its discretion agree upon with the other parties interested. 

FORM 254-AUTHORITY TO COLLECT TAX FROM PERSON WHOSE INTEREST IS LIABLE 

If the whole or any part of my estate, or the proceeds or avails thereof, shall become liable for the payment of any estate, inheritance, income or other tax, charge or assessment which my said executor shall be required to pay, said executor shall have the full power and authority, without previous notice to or demand upon any person, to pay such tax, charge or assessment out of any moneys in its hands for the account of the person whose interests hereunder are so liable. 

FORM 255-PROVISION FOR PAYMENT OF ANNUITIES FREE OF TAXES 

Each annuity given in and by this will shall be over and above and free from, any and all legacy, inheritance, or transfer tax, or taxes, duty or duties, and I direct that any and all such tax, or taxes, duty, or duties, upon, or in respect of, or upon, or in respect of, the transfer of each such annuity, shall be paid by my executors out of my estate, as part of the expense of the administration thereof, so that each annuitant shall receive the full amount of each annuity hereby given to him, or her, free from any tax, or duty.
